ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-10-sejm-49-1.u205	Thank you very much. And please take the floor of the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Minister Paul Gruza.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-10-sejm-49-1.u206	Mr. Marshal High Izbo! Ministers! The first question on taxes was asked by Mrs Sobeck. The answer seems to be simple. The question is whether the systemic changes introduced in the current laws amending PIT and CIT tax lead to the sealing of this system. The answer is complicated, because in terms of PIT, the Act introduces reliefs for the least earners, which leads to a decrease in tax revenues from this title. However, the increase in the macro base of this tax results in the summa summarum of revenue projected next year being higher than the current one. With regard to CIT, we both have a mix of sealing solutions - and they are valuablely dominant - and we provide for reliefs and exemptions within the framework of the Responsible Development Strategy for some economic solutions, such as the innovative economy, so that systemic changes also contain elements concerning the fragmentation of this tax. As regards Mrs Kołodziej's question on the effective tax rate of PIT, I would like to remind you that the budget and revenue from this are calculated on a cash-based basis, so we have some shift and the current budgetary assumptions are the result of the changes introduced in the previous year, because the main change in PIT will be consummated when annual declarations are made in the first half of next year. Therefore, the methodological assumption contained in Mrs Kołodziej's question is wrong. As regards the general comments on the tax system in question here from the Chamber, I would like to say, firstly, that budgetary revenues are actually built on a macro basis, but I would nevertheless like to remind you that in previous terms we have seen significant increases in the macro base, while at the same time falling in budget revenue, for example, from VAT. Therefore, the allegation that Law and Justice consumes tax growth is impossible because the governments of the Civic Platform and PSL have not been able to tax-consume economic growth. As regards the case referred to several times by Members of the Civic Platform concerning the arrest of the former Director of the National Tax Administration, the honesty and respect of the accused himself also call for the simple fact that the case which the former Director is accused of concerns the times of the Civic Platform and its activities in the then Ministry of Justice and has no connection with its activities within the National Tax Administration. That's what I'd like to end with. I think I've exhausted the questions.

Well, I would like to make it polite to clarify that the special provisions are actually an instrument of budget flexibility, and at the stage of budget planning we plan a special reserve when there is no possibility at this stage of planning to allocate these funds. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, I can give you many examples here, but as far as reserve 4 is concerned, the reserve popularly referred to as disaster, i.e. the one for which measures are foreseen to address the effects of natural disasters, these measures are usually planned in reserve for obvious reasons. The same is true of reserves, which I would say very often, have a social purpose when, at the planning stage or at the planning stage, we are not able to answer precisely the question of what part of a given reserve we should include in part, for example. individual voivodships. These measures are therefore planned in reserve. Certain specific provisions of the relevant laws also require the Minister of Finance to plan resources in reserves. I can mention here, for example, a reserve of 65, i.e. MdM subsidies, or a reserve of 70, where we have funds for the development of municipal and district infrastructure. The measures are planned in reserve because the relevant provisions simply impose such an obligation on us. What is more, I would like to say that in the 2018 Budget Act, we have tried where possible to include resources in some of the directors. Here I can give an example of reserve 34, i.e. Family benefits reserve, for which we immediately included more than 0.5 billion in 2018 into the voivodship budget, or an example of a reserve of 25 for social assistance, for which we immediately planned over 130 million in part of the voivodship budget. Such an example, which has already sounded, namely Minister Szrot mentioned it, is also that we included in the budget of the Chancellery of the Prime Minister the measures which were traditionally planned in the reserves, but because these measures are of a cyclical nature, we considered that they should be included. These are funds to finance the KPRM's subordinate units, i.e. the National School of Public Administration, the Polish Space Agency and the Centre for Eastern Studies. All of this has been included in the budgetary part of the authorising officer, recognising as the right thing to do in the debate, that such measures should, if possible, be included. As regards the non-use of reserves or the non-disposal of reserves as intended and re-assignment - what happened in the budgetary debate - I just want to say this on the example of the statistics that I have traced to myself in the meantime. Well, if we consider even this short period, i.e., the years 2011 to 2015, we had on average in the special reserves measures that were not used as intended, i.e. the so-called. savings or measures that could be re-trained, ranging from nearly 4 billion, 3.9 billion in 2011 to PLN 4.5 billion in 2014. Therefore, what was referred to here in terms of the Committee on Public Finance, that this year the reserves for the amount of about PLN 1.5 billion have been redefined, does not prove anything unique in the context of the non-use of reserves as intended, i.e. the reassignment of reserves for another purpose. It merely shows that we would like to use these funds for those objectives which are considered to be priorities and whose implementation or need for implementation arises during the financial year. And we see that in the reserves, the dispatchers declare some savings. If there are any further questions, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to declare the answer to you in writing. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-10-sejm-49-1.u232	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the opportunity to present a government bill on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal. The purpose of the government draft law is to compensate to some extent, within the scope of the State budget, for losses incurred by pensioners from mining companies who received free coal in kind or in cash equivalent. These persons received free coal on the basis of collective agreements, agreements and other regulations in force in a mining undertaking which had ceased to be in force before the date of entry into force of the Act as a result of agreements or terminations concluded. The purpose of the Act is to be achieved by paying compensation for the permanent loss of the right to free coal from the budget of the State. I would like to remind you that the deteriorating financial situation of mining companies in recent years has led to the need for a decision to introduce drastic financial savings in terms of their functioning. One of the decisions taken by the coal companies at that time was the limitation of mining rights. Mining companies began to express to pensioners the right to their coal debt. These decisions were taken either unilaterally by the employer's termination of collective agreements guaranteeing this right or by agreement of both parties, i.e. the social side and the employer. These measures have improved in some part of the financial situation of mining companies, but at the same time have contributed to the diversification of allowances for free coal among pensioners and miners depending on the status of the mine from which they retired or retired. According to the draft law, the entitled persons are pensioners who, at the time of the loss of entitlement, fulfilled the following cumulative conditions: they had a fixed right to a pension for incapacity for work, they received a pension, they received free coal on the basis of collective agreements, agreements and other regulations in force in the mining undertaking, the said collective agreements, agreements and other regulations expired before the date of entry into force of the bill submitted as a result of agreements concluded or terminations made. Widows, widows and orphans with a fixed right to a survivor's pension after an entitled retirement pension are also among eligible persons. The compensation shall not be granted to pensioners whose claims under the right to free coal are met on the basis of a final judgment, and to pensioners from mines wound up before 1 January 2007, as on the basis of their right to free coal, the ZUS pays them a cash equivalent under the Law of 7 September 2007 on the functioning of hard coal mining. In the case of both groups, it was assumed that their situation through the satisfaction of claims by the Social Security Office or through the implementation of a final judgment would make their position privileged. The compensation shall not be granted to pensioners who have retired after the date on which collective agreements, agreements and other regulations have ceased to apply as a result of agreements concluded or statements made in respect of the right to free coal granted to pensioners and pensioners. The compensation amounts to 10,000. zł. The compensation shall be paid once by post or by transfer to the bill indicated in the application by the paying undertaking. According to the provisions contained in the bill, the amount of compensation will not be deducted or enforced. The amount of compensation will not constitute a basis for the assessment of health insurance contributions and will also be exempt from personal income tax. The government's proposal is for pensioners to receive - in quotes - 10 PLN per hand. The government in the draft submitted also proposed that the amount of compensation should not be included in the income referred to in the Act of 21 June 2001 on housing allowances and the Act of 12 March 2004 on social assistance. It's $10,000. Whereas it is a significant amount, it will not significantly improve the quality of their lives, given the low income of pensioners and their daily needs. That's why we thought we could use the formula of not charging any additional taxes, tributes, fees of 10,000. zł. So 10,000. zloty is the amount that will be transferred to the relevant account. In order to efficiently implement the payment of such a large number of compensations, because we are talking about about about about 240 thousand. persons, the act indicates paying companies. In the case of persons entitled to liquidation from mining companies or mining companies which have ceased mining of hard coal, i.e. KW SA, KHW SA and Kazimierz-Juliusz sp. z o.o., it will be an undertaking referred to in Article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 7 September 2007 on the functioning of hard coal mining, I am referring to the Company for Restructuring of Mines, and in the case of persons entitled from mining companies which on the date of entry into force of the Act are carrying out the extraction of hard coal, the companies paying will be JSW SA, Tauron Extraction and Bogdanka. This allocation has been made because the active companies primarily have a database on eligible persons and have staff resources that will ensure the smooth performance of the task, which is very important if we aim to pay compensation as soon as possible. Depending on the extent to which the mining undertaking has retired or the pension, applicants will be submitted to the relevant paying undertakings. The proposal will include all necessary information on the person entitled who, after verification by the paying companies, will be able to pay one-off compensation. The model application of the paying companies shall be published on their websites immediately after the entry into force of this Act. There will also be a broad information campaign among pensioners and pensioners on filling in and submitting applications. Paying companies will designate specific persons, indicate telephone numbers, e-mail addresses where authorised persons will be able to obtain comprehensive information and assistance. The entitled widows, widowers and orphans will additionally have to attach to the application a document confirming entitlement to compensation, e.g. the decision of the Social Security Office to determine the right to a survivor's pension. The application for compensation will have to be accompanied by a statement, the content of which is contained in the Act. The holder shall confirm the conformity of the data contained in the application and declare that the claim for the right to free coal is not satisfied on the basis of a final judgment that no action has been brought against the claim for the right to free coal before the court and, if it has been, shall indicate to the competent court. The statement also includes a clause that the entitled person will not claim the right to free coal on the basis of collective agreements, agreements and other regulations in force in the mining undertaking which have expired before the date of entry into force of the Act as a result of agreements concluded or terminations made. The paying undertaking shall pay compensation after confirmation of the entitlement to free coal by the mining undertaking from which the entitled person has retired or pensioned. Companies will work closely together in this respect to reduce to the minimum the time needed for verification of allowances. Companies that continue to extract and will be paying companies for their pensioners will have an easier task because they have their own database. The paying undertaking may refuse to pay compensation if the application is submitted by an unauthorised person. The refusal shall be made in writing, stating the reasons and sent to the person submitting the application. The refusal will be subject to an appeal to the labour and social security courts within the time limit laid down by the law, i.e. 14 days. The purpose of determining the date of entry into force of the proposed law on the day following the date of publication is to enable the payment of compensation for the loss of the right to free coal in the form of one-off compensation as soon as possible. In view of the timetable for the preparation and implementation of this task by the relevant actors, as well as the fact that those deprived of the right to free coal have been expecting unsuccessful solutions to this issue for at least 3, 4, 5, 6 years, we believe it is necessary to enter into force on the day following the date of the announcement. Therefore, the entry into force of this Act will benefit citizens as soon as possible and will help to ease the social mood of pensioners and pensioners who are awaiting this solution. In the case of cases pending before the court, a provision was made in the draft law allowing courts, as a matter of fact, to resign from the proceedings opened and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Law concerning claims for the right to free coal after receiving information from the paying undertaking on the payment of compensation. I am making a bill to the High House, which in some way is trying to fix whether it is trying to correct certain decisions that have taken place in previous years, so that in this formula we can give compensation as soon as possible to those entitled and deprived in previous years of the coal debt. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-10-sejm-49-1.u296	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government bill on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal. The bill submitted is intended to compensate, within the scope of the State budget, for the financial losses incurred by persons who have received free coal in kind or equivalent on the basis of collective agreements and related agreements and who have lost their right to free coal as a result of the termination of collective agreements and agreements relating thereto. The constantly deteriorating financial situation of mining companies led to the need for savings in mines. The decision most often taken by company boards was to limit the privileges. These activities have had a positive impact on the operation of mining companies but, in other words, have contributed to the diversification of mining rights regarding access to free coal among pensioners and pensioners depending on the status of the mine from which they left. Currently, we are dealing with a situation in which pensioners who have left the mine which has been liquidated and which, after the bankruptcy has been declared, no one has taken over, still have the right to free coal - they get carbon deposits in the form of a monetary equivalent that ZUS pays. This is where inequality arises in access to free coal in a group of former miners. Retirists and pensioners who worked in active mines were deprived of their right to free coal as a result of the termination of collective agreements and agreements related thereto. The bill provides for a one-off payment of compensation of 10 000. zł for persons who meet the criteria set out in the Act. The eligible persons may apply for compensation within 10 days of the date of entry into force of the Act. I think the ten-day period is far too short. I hope that this period will be extended during the work of the committee. I consider it appropriate to introduce the possibility of supplementing the application, providing documents, statements after prior notice of their absence by the trader. The applicant shall have 7 days to provide missing documents or to supplement the application submitted. The right to free coal, whether in nature or in the form of a monetary equivalent, was part of the remuneration of employees of mines and pensioners in Poland and it is difficult to accept that this is not the case. The force of tradition is very large and its change takes time. In view of the above, it can be said that the bill meets the expectations of the mining environment. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill.

If I do not hear opposition, I will think that the Sejm has accepted the offer. I can't hear you objecting. This concludes the agenda items scheduled for 10 October this year. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-10-sejm-49-1.u302	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On October 10, 1922, 95 years ago, the first meeting of the Sejm of Silesia and the first term of office was convened by order of the head of the Polish state, Józef Piłsudski. The Uprising of the Silesian Sejm conditionally granted by the Legislative Sejm the organic statute of the Silesian Voivodeship. The granting of a certain autonomy of the voivodship was linked to commitments made during the plebiscite period, which decided to belong part of Upper Silesia to Poland. The organic statute was adopted in the text written by the Polish Plebiscite Commission in June 1920. This neutralized the actions of the German great industrialists who sought to create a separate so-called. a free Silesian state - a country that would actually depend on Germany. Initially, the Sejm was dominated by national affairs, since 14 out of 48 clerks of this Sejm were representatives of German, minority groups, while over time these matters became less important in the face of the Chadco-End Block conflict with the growing Sanity Block. The division into Sanctuary aimed at gradual administrative unification of Upper Silesia with the Republic and a Christian-national block fighting for autonomy. The German groups, interestingly enough, had no formal connection with national- or Chadec-endec groups, also stood on the side of maintaining autonomy. Despite the sharp friction within the Chamber and the conflicts of the Christian-national majority with the voivodeship, the Silesian Sejm remained an important institution for the Polish cause in Upper Silesia. One of the expressions of this role is the monumental architecture and architectural details of the Silesian Sejm building, which stands until today. The building was built in the first term of the Silesian Sejm, in 1924 and 1926, and today serves the Marshal's office and the voivodeship office, while the meeting room in which the Silesian Members gathered became an inspiration for the architects who designed the chamber in which we are sitting today. Today, in view of the frivolous demands to establish the autonomy of Upper Silesia, which some environments present, often solely because of their particular interests, it is worth noting the most important thing: the tradition of the Silesian Sejm is here in the High House in 55 Members elected by the inhabitants of the Silesian Voivodeship. Thank you.
